Original prompt: Daily and weekly reward Hindi contribute points to chest that grant additional coins, remnants and at 100 points grant a random card. Similar to dragon traveler. Lifetime rewards contribute to a global "Knight Level" each level granting coin, remnants and card pulls

- July 29, 2026 Mission rewards gained a second layer. Claiming a mission previously paid its Siegecoins and nothing else, so a player who swept every daily objective got the same shape of reward as one who claimed a single easy one — there was no reason to finish the board. Every mission now also carries `points`, banked on claim into the pool for its period. Daily and weekly points fill a five-rung chest ladder (`MissionRewardTrack`): daily 20/40/60/80/100, weekly 200/400/600/800/1000, each chest paying Siegecoins plus Remnants and the **final** chest of each ladder additionally rolling a random card from the deck-builder catalog. Lifetime points are different in kind — they never reset, so they feed an account-wide **Knight Level** (`KnightLevelTrack`, 30 levels, `100 + (L-1)*50` points to advance) where each level pays `200 + L*50` coins, `75 + L*25` Remnants, and a card pull that becomes 3 on every fifth level. Point budgets were set so a player does not have to sweep the board to fill the bar: daily missions total 115 points plus 10 from the login reward against a 100-point ladder, weekly total 1150 against 1000. Six new lifetime missions (solo wins, spells, traps, siegelings, expedition nodes, 500 matches) were added at the same time, because the six that existed banked only 1650 points and would have capped the Knight track around level 7 with nothing left to chase. Chests and levels are **claimed**, not auto-granted: `POST /api/missions/claim-chest` (period + threshold) and `POST /api/missions/claim-knight`, which pays out every level reached since the last claim in one call. Ledger state lives on `DailyMissionProgressEntity` — `dailyPoints`/`weeklyPoints` and their claimed-threshold lists reset with the counters they sit beside, while `knightPoints` and the `claimedKnightLevel` high-water mark deliberately survive both resets. Card pulls route through `PlayerProgressionService.grantCardsWithCap` so copy caps and duplicate-to-Remnants conversion behave exactly as they do for pack pulls; that injection is `@Lazy` because `PlayerProgressionService` already depends on `DailyMissionService` and the cycle would otherwise fail context refresh (the same pattern `PlayerTitleService` uses). Firestore rows written before this change have no `claimedKnightLevel` field, so the reader defaults it to 1 rather than 0 — level 1 is the floor and treating a missing field as 0 would have paid a phantom level to every existing account. On the hub, the Daily and Weekly tabs gained the chest rail (chests positioned at their share of the max threshold, so the rail stays honest if thresholds are ever retuned unevenly) and the Lifetime tab gained a Knight Level crest with its progress bar and claim button; every mission row now shows the points it banks. Pins advanced to `home.js?v=123` and `home.css?v=117` (the latter in both `home.html` and `card-dashboard.html`).
- Verification: `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/home.js` clean; Maven compile clean. Full suite 340 tests with 9 new `DailyMissionServiceTest` cases, all green — points bank into the right pool per period (20 daily / 250 weekly / 300 knight from three claims), the login reward banks its 10, a chest pays coins and Remnants exactly once and rejects a second claim, a locked threshold and an unknown threshold and the lifetime period are all rejected, a day/week rollover clears both point pools and both chest lists while leaving `knightPoints` and `claimedKnightLevel` untouched, `claimKnightLevels` pays levels 2 and 3 together (650 coins / 275 Remnants) and then refuses, the snapshot exposes both tracks with the right unlocked/claimable flags and the card pull on the last rung, and a budget assertion fails the build if either ladder ever becomes unreachable from its period's missions. Four `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` failures in the run (`everyPaintedElementUsesItsDedicatedNotchMedallion`, `profileDashboardTrimsBattlesFavoritesAndSocialTable`, `profileFavoriteElementIconsUseCircularNotchMedallions`, `remainingElementsUseTheirDedicatedCardBacks`) are pre-existing — they fail identically on a stashed tree at this branch point, all pin `home.html`/`home.js` versions from earlier changes, and are untouched by this work. Headless Chromium drove the real `home.html` against a mocked snapshot at 390x844 and 1920x1080: the Daily tab shows 45 points with 5 chests at 20/40/60/80/100 percent, one claimed and one glowing claimable; Weekly shows 500 points with its own ladder; Lifetime shows the Knight crest at level 4 with the unclaimed bundle and a "Claim 2 levels" button; mission rows render their point chips; no horizontal page overflow at either viewport and zero page errors. Clicking the claimable chest posted `{"period":"daily","threshold":40}` to `/api/missions/claim-chest` and the crest button posted to `/api/missions/claim-knight`, at both viewports. Captures were visually inspected. Not covered: a live authenticated session against Firestore, which this environment cannot open — the persistence round-trip was verified by reading the store's own save/load path rather than against the real database.

Original prompt: When you do construction and enter that building add a menu to hud to view construction type, time and timesavers. A nice construction drawn SVG unique to each building would also be nice in the interior so it changes in some way during itv

- July 29, 2026 Construction is now visible from inside the building being worked on. Previously the only signal that a crew was busy was the `#constructionBanner` floating over the grounds; walking into Covenant Hall mid-rank-up, or the Archive mid-restore, showed the finished room with no clock, no project name, and no way to spend a time saver without backing out to Projects. The interior HUD bar gained `#interiorBuildToggle` — a pill naming the project *kind* (Keep rank / Restoration / Expansion / Storage annex / New building), the live remaining time, and a thin progress meter — shown only when a crew is actually running on the room the player is standing in. Tapping it drops `#interiorBuildMenu` from the bar (anchored to `.interior-bar`, not the stage, because the room viewport is only ~250px tall on a phone): project name, `Phase N of 4`, a phase blurb, the progress meter, and a Stage / Time left / Finishes / Crew fact grid, followed by the same material and Siegecoin time savers the Projects panel offers. `timeSaverMarkup` grew an `alwaysOpen` argument so the menu is its own disclosure rather than nesting a second collapse, and `buyConstructionTimeSaver` now re-renders the interior instead of calling `openPanel('projects')` when the purchase came from inside a room — buying time no longer ejects the player from the building they are watching. Alongside it, `#interiorBuildArt` draws a flat work-site SVG unique to each of the ten walkable rooms (hall stone gantry, woodlot saw pit and drying racks, archive shoring and shelving, garden trellis, forge flue, fridge vault lattice, generator armature, quarry derrick, kitchen oven dome, enclave rafters and nests), bottom-anchored on the room floor and capped at `min(64%, 420px)` so the room still reads through it. Each drawing is four `data-phase-min` groups; `renderInteriorConstruction` raises one more group per quarter of the build, so the same room is visibly further along on the next visit rather than only the clock having moved. `constructionTarget` was widened from a hardcoded `_level_2` check to any `_level_N` plus `_storage_annex`, which also fixes the grounds scaffolding for storage-annex projects. State: `interiorBuildOpen`, closed on every room change and on exit, and Escape closes the menu before it closes the room. Cache pins advanced to `keep.css?v=38` / `keep.js?v=39`.
- Verification: `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/keep.js` clean; `git diff --check` clean; full `./mvnw test` 333 tests, including the new `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest#enteringABuildingUnderConstructionShowsItsSiteAndAConstructionHudMenu` (HUD hooks, room resolution, kind labels, live clock hooks, per-room art map, and the stay-in-the-room speed-up path). Four `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` failures remain in the run (`everyPaintedElementUsesItsDedicatedNotchMedallion`, `profileDashboardTrimsBattlesFavoritesAndSocialTable`, `profileFavoriteElementIconsUseCircularNotchMedallions`, `remainingElementsUseTheirDedicatedCardBacks`) — all four are `home.html` cache pins, all four fail identically on a stashed tree at this branch point, and none is touched by this change. Headless Chromium drove the real `keep.html` at 390x844 and 1920x1080 against a snapshot dumped from the real `KeepService` (all six workshops, Enclave built, Hall rank 3, six construction slots): 322/322 checks, zero page or console errors. All ten rooms show the toggle with a kind and a clock, draw art tagged to their own room id with four phase groups and a distinct SVG body (no two rooms share a drawing), raise exactly `phase + 1` groups at 10/35/60/88% progress, open a menu carrying the project name, `Phase N of 4`, all four facts, and two enabled time savers, with no horizontal overflow at either viewport. A room with no crew on it shows neither toggle nor art, and walking into the worked room raises both. The clock ticks in both the toggle and the menu over two seconds without the menu being rebuilt; spending materials from inside leaves the player in `great_hall` with the panel closed and the clock cut; Escape closes the menu and leaves the room open. Captures of all four hall phases and of every room's site at both viewports were visually inspected. Not covered: a live signed-in keep — `/api/keep` requires Firestore account credentials this environment does not have, so the snapshot was generated from `KeepService` directly rather than fetched from a running server.

Original prompt: Update the earth card back

- July 29, 2026 Earth card back replaced with the navy-and-gold artwork supplied with the request. The shipped `card-back-earth.png` was the odd one out of the twelve elemental backs: its frame sat on a mossy olive field and, unlike every sibling, the art was matted on **white**, so the Stone Garden tile on `/decks` drew a white halo around its rounded corners while Blazing Core, Gale Talons, and Frostmarch read as seamless dark tiles. The new source (1024x1536) was resized to the established `520x760` footprint with Lanczos and written as both the PNG and its `.webp` twin — every local raster card asset has one, and `card-binder-visual.js` / `game.js` swap to `.webp` on supporting browsers, so shipping only the PNG would have left the old art live for most players. The new art carries the same near-black matte as the other eleven backs (corner `0,0,0`), which is what removes the halo; the leaf-over-mountain sigil and the gold diamond notch markers are unchanged in meaning, only the field behind them moves from olive to the deep blue the rest of the set uses. Both cache-bust counters that gate this art advanced together — `DECK_ART_ASSET_VERSION` in `game.js` and `ELEMENTAL_CARD_BACK_VERSION` in `home.js`, 5 → 6 — because the two bundles version the same `/img/decks/*` URLs independently and a stale one would keep serving `?v=5` from the service worker's image cache (`cacheFirstImage` matches on the full URL including query, so the bump is what evicts it). The bundles' own pins followed in every page that loads them: `game.js?v=220` in `play.html`, `home.html`, and `card-dashboard.html`, and `home.js?v=122` in `home.html`. No JS logic, CSS, or deck data changed — `scripts/storage-asset-manifest.json` is a record of a past Storage upload and is not read by the app, so it was left alone.
- Verification: `node --check` clean on both `js/game.js` and `js/home.js`. Geometry and matte asserted directly on the written files — PNG and WebP both `520x760`, both corners `0,0,0`, matching `card-back-fire.png`. A headless-Chromium pass at 390x844 and 1920x1080 served the static tree and drew the new earth back beside the fire back at deck-tile size, reading each back through a canvas: both reported `520x760` with corner `0,0,0` at both viewports, and the versioned URLs `card-back-earth.png?v=6` / `.webp?v=6` both returned 200. Captures were visually inspected — the earth tile now matches the fire tile's dark-edged framing with no white border. Not covered: the live `/decks` grid behind auth, which this environment cannot sign into; the change is asset-plus-version only, and the tile markup that consumes these paths was untouched.

Original prompt: Implement character first menu with cell selections example. Favorite singeing can be a singular cel that shows your current favorite of a an empty silhouette if none selected. You click on the favorite portrait that opens a cell selection of singeing buttons/cells. That are portraits in a grid like view. You click the new portrait a menu comes up With the paper cutout a little line about the effect and description and an approve or deny button.

- July 29, 2026 The Keeper's Favor became a character-first cell menu. Covenant Hall previously listed every owned Siegeling as a stacked name row and committed the keep-wide honor on the first tap, so a mis-scroll on a phone could silently swap the bonus that drives every station's output. The panel now opens on a single honored cell — the current favorite drawn with the shared paper-cutout treatment, or a Siegeling-shaped silhouette when nobody is honored — with the effect stated beside it. Tapping the cell replaces the card body with `.favor-grid`, an `auto-fill` grid of portrait cells (one per owned Siegeling, each carrying its own bonus and a star on whoever is honored). Tapping a portrait raises `#favorOverlay`, a confirm sheet holding the candidate's paper cutout, one effect line (`+16% keep-wide output`), a description naming who steps down and whether rapport already multiplies the favor, and Approve/Deny buttons; Escape and the backdrop both deny. Approving the Siegeling already honored is the step-down path, so the sheet reverses its wording (`Ends +16%…`, button `Step down`) rather than needing a second control. Nothing is committed until Approve — the honored cell keeps the same footprint in every state so the card beneath a thumb never shifts. The previewed percentage is served, not recomputed: `serializeResident` now emits `favoriteBonusPercent` per resident from the same `favoriteBoostFor` helper `favoriteBoost` uses, so rapport folds in identically and the preview cannot drift from the boost the keep receives (the client's rarity ladder survives only as a fallback for older payloads). The old `data-set-favorite` handler and every `.favorite-choice`/`.favorite-current` rule are gone. Cache pins advanced to `keep.css?v=36` / `keep.js?v=37` — one past the `?v=35`/`?v=36` that the parallel Enclave-collapse change (#559) landed on `main` while this was in review, since both changes edit the same two bundles.
- Verification: full Maven suite green, 326/326, including a new `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest#keepersFavorPicksACharacterCellAndConfirmsBeforeHonoring` (cell + silhouette, portrait grid, confirm overlay with cutout/approve/deny, and an explicit failure if `data-set-favorite` or `.favorite-choice` returns) and a widened `KeepServiceTest#favoriteSieglingGrantsRarityScaledKeepWideBoost` asserting each resident previews its own bonus (Common 5%, Legendary 20%, Common at rapport 1 = 6%, matching the applied boost). `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/keep.js` clean. Headless Chromium drove the real `keep.html` through `__KEEP_TEST_SNAPSHOT__` at 390x844 and 1920x1080: 66/66 checks, zero page or console errors — empty cell shows the silhouette and no legacy list; tapping it opens 5 portrait cells (3 columns at both sizes) all using the paper treatment; picking Legendary Pylord shows the cutout, `+22% keep-wide output`, and a description quoting its rapport, with the favorite still unset; Deny closes the sheet and leaves the grid untouched; Approve commits Frostkin, returns to the honored cell reading `+16% to every station's output…`, and holds the cell height within 0.3px of the empty state; reopening stars the current favorite and its sheet reads `Ends +16% keep-wide output` / `Step down`, which clears back to the silhouette; Escape denies without committing; no horizontal overflow at either viewport. Captures in the session scratchpad (`favor-empty/grid/confirm/honored` at both viewports) were visually inspected. Re-verified after merging `main`: #559 collapsed the Enclave into its own cell grid and touched `closePanel`, the keep pins, and the same regression test, so the conflicts were resolved by keeping both guards (the favor menu closes *and* the Enclave collapses on panel close) and advancing the pins past main's; the two feature CSS namespaces (`.favor-*` / `.enclave-space-*`) do not collide. Post-merge: full suite 326/326 and the 66-check headless run repeated green on the merged tree, plus a focused pass confirming #559's behavior survives the shared `closePanel` resolution — the Enclave opens as 5 space cells, one expands to a single space card, and closing the panel collapses it back to 5 cells with no page errors. Also refreshed three stale `home.js?v=120` pin assertions to the `?v=121` that `#558` actually shipped — they were already failing on this branch's base and are unrelated to this change.

Original prompt: In the keep siegeling enclave make the enclave spots collapsible. The menu should feature buttons of the siegelings opening a single menu for each enclave spot that you can close retrying back to the cell like button menu

- July 29, 2026 Enclave spaces collapse to a cell-like button menu. The Enclave surface (panel and walkable interior alike) rendered all five spaces as stacked `.enclave-slot-card`s, each with a portrait, a rapport meter, and every task that resident offers — on a phone that buried spaces 3-5 under several screens of scrolling, and the assign menu could be open on a card the player had scrolled away from. `enclaveMarkup` now has two states driven by the new `state.enclaveOpenSlot` (-1 = collapsed): collapsed renders a three-column grid of `.enclave-space-cell` buttons, one per space, showing the Siegeling's cutout, name, element, rapport level, and a green badge counting tasks ready to bank (or a dashed "Open space" seat when empty); tapping a cell expands that space alone through the existing `enclaveSlotMarkup`, and the `.enclave-collapse` close button in the card header — `data-enclave-space="-1"` — returns to the grid. Only one space is ever expanded, so the assign menu, rapport meter, and task list keep their existing markup and behaviour untouched inside it. `collapseEnclaveSpaces()` resets both the open space and the picker whenever the player leaves the surface (`closePanel`, `closeInterior`, and switching panels), so reopening the Enclave always lands on the grid rather than inside a space left behind. The debug snapshot gained `enclave.openSlot` / `enclave.spacesCollapsed`. Cache pins advanced to `keep.css?v=35` / `keep.js?v=36`.
- Verification: `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/keep.js` clean; `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest#enclaveSwapsResidentsByPortraitAndTheDashboardCanTuneTheKeep` extended to pin the collapsed grid, the two `data-enclave-space` directions, `collapseEnclaveSpaces`, and the new CSS, and it passes along with the advanced pins in `keepBuildingsAndRoomsUseLayeredPaperTreatments`. - July 27, 2026 Siegling size bands now apply in every room, not just the Enclave. The band shipped in #537 only reached `.enclave-residents` and `.enclave-interior-residents`, so a Gigantic legendary towered over the enclave huts and then shrank back to a stock silhouette the moment it was posted to a workshop — the same creature read as two different sizes depending on where it stood. The `--cutout-scale` ladder (.84 / 1 / 1.24 / 1.62) is now declared once for every surface that draws a Siegeling at world scale: the enclave exterior and interior, the grounds worker at the Woodlot hotspot, the `.lodge-resident` (Woodlot and Covenant Hall), and the `.facility-room-resident` in all six workshops. Every cutout dimension in those rooms consumes the scale — both the `has-overlay-art` silhouette and the initials paper token, at base and narrow-viewport sizes — so the ladder cannot be flattened by a phone media query. No JS change was needed: `setResidentOverlayArt` already stamped `data-size` on every node it paints, which is why the bug was CSS-only. `.shrine-art` was deliberately left alone: `#favoriteShrine` was removed from the grounds in the July 21 change and the Covenant Hall shows the favorite as a panel chooser chip rather than a world-scale cutout, so scaling it would only have dressed up dead CSS. Two collisions the growth introduced were fixed at the same time: on a phone a Gigantic cutout reached the interior next-room arrow in both the workshop rooms and the lodge, so `.facility-room-resident` moves to `right: 22%` and `.lodge-resident` to `left: 46%` on narrow viewports — a fixed nudge for every band rather than a per-band offset, which would make a Siegeling hop sideways when reassigned. Cache pins advanced to `keep.css?v=33` and `keep.js?v=34` (re-advanced past main during the merge below).
- Verification: full Maven suite green, 320/320, including a new `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` case that fails the build if any of the five world-scale holders loses a size band, if a room's cutout dimensions stop consuming `var(--cutout-scale)`, or if the dead `.shrine-art` rules are scaled. Headless Chromium walked all seven production rooms via the interior rail at 390x844 and 1920x1080 against a snapshot with a resident of a known band posted to each: 22/22 both as paper tokens and as art cutouts. Desktop cutouts measured 96 px MEDIUM, 119 px LARGE, 155.5 px GIGANTIC (ratios 1.240 and 1.620 against the .84/1/1.24/1.62 ladder); phone measured 78 / 96.7 / 126.4 px with the same ratios. A band renders identically in every workshop (garden and kitchen both 155.5 px; fridge and quarry both 96 px), and the grounds worker carries the Woodlot resident's band. The 42/42 Enclave harness still passes unchanged. The two arrow collisions were found by inspecting the phone captures and confirmed by hit-testing `document.elementFromPoint` at each arrow's centre — the arrows stayed clickable throughout (they paint above the art), so the defect was cosmetic, and after the nudge the bounding boxes no longer intersect at either viewport. Room captures at both sizes were visually inspected.
- Merge with `main` (16 commits, every touched file overlapping): a parallel session had meanwhile scaled `.lodge-resident` and `.facility-room-resident` and added a new world-scale `.hall-favorite-resident` in the Covenant Hall, so most of this branch dissolved into main's version. What survived is the union: main's block plus `.resident-worker > span`, the grounds worker, which main still did not scale — a Siegeling changed height merely by being seen from outside its workshop. The phone arrow-collision nudges also survived, and they are not hypothetical: removing them on the merged tree reproduces the overlap against main's own geometry (garden -28px, woodlot -12px), so main currently ships gigantic residents tucked behind the next-room arrow on a phone. Cache pins were re-advanced past main's (`keep.css?v=32`->33, `keep.js?v=33`->34) since both sides edited the same bundles, and the regression test was widened to cover `.hall-favorite-resident` while keeping the assertion that the orphaned `.shrine-art` rules stay unscaled. Post-merge verification: full suite 324/324, and a headless walk of all seven rooms plus the Covenant Hall at 390x844 and 1920x1080 passed 24/24 — the grounds worker now carries its band (95.5px phone / 118.1px desktop at GIGANTIC), the hall favorite scales (127.2 / 156.3px), every room reports its expected band, and both arrow clearances are positive at both viewports.

The track became a vertical flex column (`flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0; overflow-y: auto`) so it owns the card's leftover height instead of overflowing it, and `.journey-nodes` is now an `auto-fill` grid that wraps a chapter's levels into rows. Portrait tightens node padding, badges, and type (94px columns) so a whole chapter fits without scrolling; the short-landscape block drops the header blurb and chapter subtitles, shrinks the chrome, and caps the card at `100dvh` minus the safe area. Cache pins advanced to `keep.css?v=31` / `keep.js?v=32`.

Original prompt: Singeing enclave should display Siegelings that are not assigned. If they are show a reassign tag,  in enclsve to swap out active Siegelings implementing Tap the image instead of remove button so tapping the singling image on the left will reopen the assign menu. Siegelings can offer different task in the enclsve to build you rapport with them. Building rapport increases their buffs by a certain threshold. Keeping the rarity disparity that will also increase by that threshold. Give them unique task based on the Siegeling themselves with defaults based on their elements. Add a Keep section to the dashboard for updating building effects, singling effects buffs decorations etc

- July 27, 2026 Enclave rapport, portrait swapping, and a Keep page on the card dashboard. **Assignment**: the Enclave picker used to list every owned Siegeling as an undifferentiated grid, so moving a worker off the Forge looked identical to inviting an idle one. `KeepService` now publishes an `assignment` block on every serialized resident (`STATION`/`ENCLAVE`, its id, and the room's display name), and the picker groups by it — "Unassigned · N" first, then "Already assigned" where each chip carries a pink `Reassign · <post>` tag naming exactly what the player would be pulling that Siegeling off. **Swapping**: the per-slot `Clear` button and `<details>` disclosure are gone; the resident portrait *is* the control (a 56x62 button with a SWAP ribbon and `aria-expanded`), and the open picker is tracked in `state.enclavePickerSlot` rather than the DOM because every snapshot refresh re-renders the panel body. Choosing the seated resident is the "let them leave" action, still offered as an explicit button. Because the Enclave is reachable both as a side panel and as a walkable interior — two different containers for the same `buildingMarkup` — the picker toggle redraws through a new `rerenderActiveSurface()`; calling `renderPanel()` alone silently did nothing inside the interior. **Rapport**: each Enclave resident offers a three-rung task ladder (`KeepEnclaveTaskCatalog`) — two rungs from its element and one personal bond task selected deterministically from the card id, so two Fire Siegelings never offer the same set — and tasks repeat: banking one resets its progress, increments a completion count, and pays rapport, gold, and Remnants without entering the one-shot `keepRewardClaimIds` set. Rapport levels 0-5 (thresholds 0/5/12/22/36/55) produce one multiplier that scales *every* buff that resident already grants: its workshop affinity bonus and, when favorited, its rarity-tiered keep-wide bonus. Because the favorite bonus is tiered (5/8/12/16/20%), multiplying it widens the Common-to-Legendary gap as rapport grows instead of flattening it. New progress buckets `CONSTRUCTION`, `DECORATION`, and `CONVERSATION` join the existing timber/material/crafting ones so element ladders can ask for more than resource grinding. `enclave_mission:<id>` still resolves (to the resident's first task) for a cached client. **Dashboard**: a new Keep page (`keep-admin.js`, `/api/keep/tuning`, Firestore doc `appConfig/keepTuning` with a project-file fallback) tunes workshop output and storage, every construction project's timber cost and duration, all resident buff percentages including the rapport step and level thresholds, all 73 tool/decoration blueprints (name, effect label, material costs), and the Enclave task ladders per element or per card id. Every field is an override — blank means the shipped value, shown as the input's placeholder — so an empty document reproduces today's balance exactly and a malformed one degrades to it rather than breaking a live keep. Cache pins advanced to `keep.css?v=25`, `keep.js?v=28`, `card-dashboard.css?v=37`, `card-dashboard.js?v=52`, `dashboard-info.js?v=5` — one past the pins the size-tag change below shipped, since both branches touched the same four bundles and a returning browser would otherwise keep the earlier copy.
- Verification: full Maven suite green, 318/318, including eleven new tests. `KeepServiceTest` gains three: the element ladders differ by element and the bond task differs by card id while task copy names its Siegeling; the Enclave payload reports Emberling at the "Restorative Woodlot" and Mossling in "Enclave space 1"; banking a task pays rapport, resets progress, and repeats, and at rapport 1 the woodlot rate moves 1.15x1.05 -> 1.165x1.055 while the Common favorite goes 5% -> 6% and the Legendary 20% -> 22% (the gap widening, not closing). `KeepTuningServiceTest` and `KeepTuningControllerTest` cover partial documents, negative/descending input being repaired rather than trusted, the storage round trip, a Firestore outage degrading to defaults, the editor guard rejecting an unauthenticated save without touching the live document, and every element shipping a valid ladder. `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` now fails the build if the portrait swap, the reassign tag, the rapport markup, or the dashboard Keep wiring regress, or if a `Clear` button returns. `node --check` clean on `keep.js`, `keep-admin.js`, `card-dashboard.js`, `dashboard-info.js`. Headless Chromium at 390x844 and 1920x1080 drove the real `keep.html` against a snapshot dumped from `KeepService` (three seated residents, one finished task, mid-ladder rapport): 38/38 checks — three tasks per resident with the personal one tagged, the finished one bankable and the rest disabled, no stray remove button, tapping the portrait opening the menu, "Unassigned · 3" listed before four `Reassign` chips naming the Woodlot/Garden/Enclave posts, and the chosen Siegeling seated and re-reported as assigned, with zero horizontal overflow or page errors. A second run against the live backend exercised the dashboard Keep page at both viewports: 40/40 — six workshops, 25 projects, and 73 blueprints listed with shipped placeholders, the rapport step and thresholds editable, element ladders read-only until "Start from shipped", an unauthenticated save refused with the server's own message, and the edited ladder reaching the wire as a Keep tuning document. Screenshots of the Enclave, the open assign menu, and the dashboard page were inspected at both sizes; two defects found that way were fixed — the reassign tag was ellipsing away the post name in the three-column chip grid (now wraps), and the Keep panel was squeezed into the deck workspace's 320px browse column (now spans every column). Re-verified after merging the Siegling size-tag change below, which touched the same `enclaveMarkup` line, `Resident` record, and four cache pins: the slot card keeps its size band, interior cutouts still measure 77/114/149 px across Small/Large/Gigantic, and the rapport picker is unaffected — 42/42 at both viewports, full suite 319/319.

Original prompt: In enclave larger Siegelings should have larger cutouts. In dashboard add small, medium, large and gigantic tags. Initially assign these by evolution phase however all Legendary Siegelings are Gigantic by default. In the dashboard I'll make one off changes. But default it: Common and uncommon - small; Rare and epic - medium and Large; Legendary - gigantic

- July 27, 2026 Siegling size tags and scaled enclave cutouts: Siegelings now carry a size band (`SMALL`/`MEDIUM`/`LARGE`/`GIGANTIC`) that decides how tall their paper cutout stands in the Keep enclave, so a legendary towers over a base-form common instead of every resident sharing one silhouette. New `SieglingSize` enum with `defaultFor(rarity, evolutionDepth)`: Common/Uncommon small, Rare medium, Epic large, Legendary gigantic — rarity is the band because it already tracks how far along an evolution line a card sits, and evolution depth only decides rows that carry no rarity at all (base small, second stage medium, final stage large). `ManualSieglingCatalog.applyOverrides` fills the default in after evolution names resolve, so every catalog consumer reads a concrete size; an explicit `size` key in the override JSON or a Firestore dashboard row wins, and an unknown value is rejected at publish time rather than silently ignored. `SieglingCard` carries the field through `copy()`, `KeepService` serializes it onto each resident (residents are evolution-line roots, hence depth 0), and `keep.js` stamps `data-size` on the cutout with a rarity fallback for older payloads. `keep.css` turns that into a `--cutout-scale` (.84 / 1 / 1.24 / 1.62) applied to the exterior hut cutouts, the interior room cutouts, and the narrow-viewport interior override, with larger silhouettes stacking in front so a gigantic neighbour is never clipped; the enclave slot card now reads "Fire · Common · Small". The dashboard's Siegeling page gained a **Size** select next to Preferred Row whose first option is `Auto (…)` showing the derived band — picking a size writes the `size` key for that one card, leaving it on Auto omits the key so the card keeps following its rarity — plus a size tag in the browser list meta line and a `siegling-size` info topic. Cache pins advanced to `keep.css?v=24`, `keep.js?v=27`, `card-dashboard.js?v=51`, `dashboard-info.js?v=4`.
- Verification: full Maven suite green, 307/307, including two new `ManualSieglingCatalogTest` cases — one asserting every generated Fire card lands on its rarity band, that every legendary is gigantic, and that an explicit `"size":"gigantic"` on a common survives merge, `copy()`, and the override-file round trip; one asserting an unknown size (`HUGE`) fails `validateDefinitions` before it can reach the live catalog. `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` tracks the new keep pins. `node --check` clean on `keep.js`, `card-dashboard.js`, and `dashboard-info.js`. Headless Chromium against the real `keep.html` via `__KEEP_TEST_SNAPSHOT__` with five residents (small/medium/large/gigantic plus a legendary carrying no size at all) at 390x844 and 1920x1080: exterior cutouts measured 31.9 / 38 / 47.1 / 61.5 px tall and interior cutouts 55.4 / 66 / 81.8 / 106.9 px (mobile) and 77.3 / 92 / 114.1 / 149 px (desktop), the size-less legendary matched the gigantic band through the client fallback, no page errors and no horizontal overflow at either viewport; the interior capture was visually inspected. A second harness drove the real `card-dashboard.html` against a mocked editor payload: the browser list shows `Common | Small`, `Rare | Medium`, `Epic | Large`, `Legendary | Gigantic` and `Common | Gigantic` for a pinned card, the select offers `Auto (Small…Gigantic)` matching each card's rarity, the JSON preview omits `size` on Auto and emits `"size":"LARGE"` after a pick, and changing a card's rarity from Uncommon to Legendary moved its Auto label from `Auto (Small)` to `Auto (Gigantic)` — zero page errors.

Original prompt: Player reported having to login twice. Once to the website and again to the Keep mode. Review and fix all login persistence issues and any delays to login in when you first access the app. It should remain logged in accross all modes and not require a relog.

- July 27, 2026 login persistence across every mode: fixed the reported "log in twice — once to the website, again to My Keep". Root cause was a false proof of cookie auth. Firebase Hosting forwards **no cookie except `__session`** to Cloud Run, so the httpOnly `sgl_session` credential was stripped at the edge and never reached the backend in production; meanwhile the client-readable `sgl_auth` flag cookie *was* stored by the browser, and `home.js`/`game.js`/`keep.js`/`landing.js` all read it as "cookies round-trip here" and discarded the real Bearer token from localStorage. The login response itself carries the profile, so the page the player signed in on looked fine — every later full-page navigation (My Keep, Play, Siege, a hub reload) arrived unauthenticated. Fixes: (1) `SessionCookieService` now issues the session as `__session` (the only name Hosting forwards) scoped to `Path=/api` so it never enters the Hosting cache key for static assets, and expires the pre-rename `sgl_session` alongside it; `SessionCookieAuthFilter` reads `__session` first and still accepts `sgl_session`. (2) The client no longer guesses: the filter publishes whether a session cookie actually arrived, `/api/auth/me` returns a verified `cookieSession` flag, and all four bundles keep the real token until the *server* confirms the cookie reached it (persisted as `sieglingsCookieAuthConfirmed`). If cookies are ever stripped again, auth degrades to the Bearer header instead of a forced re-login. (3) `adventure.js` tested the sentinel with an `indexOf` prefix that never matched the literal `'cookie'`, so Siege sent `Authorization: Bearer cookie` — a bogus header that beat the server's cookie bridge and hid the account from every expedition; it now compares the whole value. (4) My Keep no longer answers a transient failure with a login form: `KeepController` maps an unexpected runtime failure to 503 instead of letting it escape as a 500, and `keep.js` shows the sign-in gate only for an authoritative 401, offering "Try again" otherwise. (5) First-access latency: `/api/auth/me` (a dozen fan-out Firestore reads) was fired twice per page load because init and the pageshow/focus listeners raced; both `syncProfile()` and `syncAuthProfile()` now collapse concurrent callers onto one in-flight request. Cache pins advanced to `game.js?v=213`, `home.js?v=116`, `keep.js?v=26`, `adventure.js?v=42`, `landing.js?v=17` (index) / `v=19` (landing).
- Verification: full Maven suite green, 305/305, including six new tests — `AuthControllerTest` covers `cookieSession` true/false/unauthenticated, the `__session` name + `Path=/api` + HttpOnly/Secure attributes, and the filter bridging both the new and pre-rename cookie names; `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` now fails the build if any bundle re-introduces the `hasReadableAuthCookie() && !isStandalonePWA()` guess, if `adventure.js` returns to the prefix test, or if My Keep gates on a non-401. `node --check` clean on all five touched bundles. Behaviourally verified in headless Chromium against a harness that reproduces the production topology — an edge that strips every cookie but `__session` in front of a backend implementing the same auth contract: sign in once on the landing modal, then My Keep loads with the gate hidden, Siege authenticates as the same account and never sends `Bearer cookie`, and `/api/auth/me` still reports authenticated with `cookieSession: true` after full-page navigation — 14/14 checks at 390x844 and 1920x1080. The same harness run against pre-fix code reproduces the bug exactly (My Keep gate visible, real token discarded), and a stripped-cookie scenario confirms the new fallback keeps My Keep working when the cookie never arrives. `/api/auth/me` per page load measured 2 -> 1 on the Play page.

- July 26, 2026 Siege expedition menu rebuilt on current `main` from the useful intent of stale PR #393 without importing its superseded map-scroll rewrite. The active-run HUD now opens one accessible Save / Restart / Quit dialog across maps, battles, and encounters. Save forces a durable server checkpoint and reports failure without pretending the run is safe; Quit saves first and remains in place if persistence fails; Restart abandons the server checkpoint before clearing local state, so a failed request cannot orphan an invisible run. Added `/api/siege/run/save`, advanced `adventure.css` to `v43` and `adventure.js` to `v41`, and added service/static regressions for persistence, cache pins, operation ordering, and the absence of `resetPageScroll`.

A focused Chromium flow at 390x844 and 1440x900 verified initial focus, Save failure/retry/success, Restart failure/retry/success, and Save-before-Quit: failed writes kept the token and open menu, successful restart cleared local state only after the second server response, Quit made one save request before `/play`, both layouts had zero horizontal overflow, and no unexpected page/console errors appeared. Both menu screenshots were visually inspected. The complete Maven suite passes 296/296.

Original prompt: Include these Use these for undead, light and shadow.

- July 25, 2026 Undead, Light, and Shadow notch medallions: completed the painted notch set. `UNDEAD` and `LIGHT` had still been falling through `notchIconPath`'s fallback to their flat `/img/elements/element-*.svg` chips and are now registered in all three copies of `NOTCH_ICON_PATHS` (`js/game.js`, `js/home.js`, `js/card-binder-visual.js`); `SHADOW`, one of the original five coins, was replaced with the new crescent art. All twelve painted elements now carry dedicated medallions and only `NEUTRAL` still uses the SVG fallback. Assets were cut with the same pipeline as the previous pass -- these sources are RGB with the transparency checkerboard baked in, so the coin mask comes from colour (saturated or dark = coin, light neutral = checker), then a least-squares rim fit drives a square-of-side-2R crop with the rim landing 1px inside the canvas edge so the antialiased circular alpha mask cuts through coin pixels rather than blending checker into a light halo. Fits landed at 0.40-0.93px mean residual on radii of 354-436px. `style.css` gained the `.notch-dot.light` / `.bc-notch.filled.light` colour rules it was missing (`--light` with a darker rim), matching the Poison pair added earlier; Undead already had its own. Cache pins advanced to `style.css?v=216`, `game.js?v=212`, `card-binder-visual.js?v=17` (`home.html`, `play.html`, `card-dashboard.html`) and `home.js?v=114` (`home.html`).
- Verification: `node --check` passes on all three touched bundles; focused Maven `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` passes 23/23 (no Java changed). The headless-Chromium harness, extended to all twelve painted elements, loaded the real `play.html` at 390x844 and 1920x1080 and asserted against live `notchIconStyle`/`notchIconPath` and real `.notch-dot` elements: every element resolves to `/img/notches/notch-<element>.png` with no SVG fallback, each medallion decodes at 256x256, each dot's computed `background-image` is its own coin, no `/img/notches/` request returned non-200, and the dots keep their 50% radius at both viewports (6px mobile, 11px desktop). A pixel probe over the outer 6px ring of the three new chips found 0-53 near-white low-saturation pixels out of 4640 (gold rim specular highlights, not checker bleed; Light was exactly 0). Fits landed at 0.31-1.61px mean residual on radii of 344-472px. The rim is scaled to land 1px inside the canvas edge so the antialiased circular alpha mask cuts through coin pixels instead of blending the checkerboard into a light halo. `style.css` needed no change this pass (`.notch-dot.psychic` / `.bc-notch.filled.psychic` already existed) so its pin stays at `?v=215`; `game.js?v=211`, `card-binder-visual.js?v=16` (`home.html`, `play.html`, `card-dashboard.html`) and `home.js?v=113` (`home.html`) advanced.

Original prompt: Implement these notches for metal, water electric and poison.

The supplied 1024px source art was cropped by least-squares fitting the medallion rim from its alpha mask and cutting a square of side 2R around that centre, because the dots render as `border-radius:50%` chips with `background-size:cover` — the medallion circle has to be inscribed exactly in the square canvas or the art drifts inside the dot. Everything outside the fitted circle is masked transparent, which also removes the shield/droplet points that poke past the rim on the Metal, Water, and Poison medallions and would otherwise be clipped mid-shape. Poison also gained the `.notch-dot.poison` / `.bc-notch.filled.poison` colour rules it was missing (`--poison` with a darker rim), so the no-icon fallback matches its siblings. Cache pins advanced to `style.css?v=215`, `game.js?v=210`, `card-binder-visual.js?v=15` (`home.html`, `play.html`, `card-dashboard.html`) and `home.js?v=112` (`home.html`).

Original prompt: The visual on portrait is cut off. For all events with SVG backgrounds make sure there is a landscape and a portrait format that fits those orientations. On mobile landscape the options should not require scroll scale everything so it fits on one screen. After battles let's revert the evolution again. But in rewards make sure to sometimes offer the item that allows features to evolve at the beginning of battle.

- July 26, 2026 Siege orientation art, landscape fit, evolution revert, and sigil rewards: four fixes across the expedition mode. (1) **Portrait stage art** — every illustrated location (Rest Camp, Buried Cache, Broker, Smith, Caravan, Event) drew a single `viewBox="0 0 1200 680"` board with `preserveAspectRatio="slice"`, so a tall phone stage cropped ~40% of the width away and the scene read as an empty gradient with the prop half off-frame. Each stage now ships a second `720x1280` composition (`.location-art-tall`, portrait-suffixed gradient/filter ids so the two SVGs in the DOM cannot cross-wire) drawn for the tall frame: sky and far scenery up top, the hero prop right-of-centre clear of the party figures, the horizon above the glass overlay. `@media (orientation:portrait)` swaps which one is in flow. Hero props sit inside x∈[80,640] so the ~68-unit-per-side slice crop on the narrowest phones still cannot clip them. (2) **Landscape fits one screen** — the `max-width:760px` choice carousels were width-only rules authored for portrait and leaked into short landscape viewports, and the short-landscape query turned the camp service menu into another horizontal scroller. The carousels are now scoped to `(orientation:portrait)`, and a new landscape query lays every choice row out as one flex line (`flex:1 1 0`) that divides the overlay evenly at any option count, clips instead of scrolling, and scales card glyphs/titles/descriptions down with a 3-line clamp. (3) **Evolution reverts after battle** — `snapshotCombatant` never persisted `evolvedFrom` (or `itemId`), so a run resumed from a mid-battle checkpoint came back with the evolved form and no chain to walk; `clearBattleBuffs` then had nothing to revert to and the evolution became permanent. Both fields now round-trip. (4) **Sigil rewards** — `generateRewards` only built CARD/RECRUIT/UPGRADE, so the Evolution Sigil was reachable only from shops. A new `ITEM` reward kind offers a sigil after 22% of normal wins and 40% of elite wins, gated on somebody in the warband actually being able to equip it and none already spare, with the stage-3 sigil as the rarer 25% upgrade. The reward grid switched from a hard 3-column track to auto columns so the resulting 4-option spread stays on one row. Cache pins advanced to `adventure.css?v=40` and `adventure.js?v=40`.
- Verification: `./mvnw test` passes **289/289** (zero failures/errors/skips), including two new suites — `SiegeSigilRewardTest` (an evolving warband is offered a sigil; the pick lands in the pack and equips; a warband with no evolution path is never offered a dead sigil) and a new `SiegeEvolutionSigilTest` case that evolves via sigil, round-trips the live battle through `snapshotBattle`/`restoreBattle` exactly as a resume does, rebuilds the party from the restored combatants, then wins — asserting the party comes home in its base form. That case was confirmed to **fail on the unfixed snapshot code** (`the pre-evolution form survives the round trip ==> expected: not <null>`) before the fix landed. `node --check js/adventure.js` passes, all six portrait SVGs parse as valid XML, and CSS braces balance. A headless-Chromium harness drove the real `adventure.html`/`adventure.css` with injected screen state at 390x844 portrait and 844x390 landscape across all six locations plus the reward screen: the orientation-matched art is the one painted in each case (portrait uses the 720x1280 board), and in landscape every choice row reports zero horizontal overflow, a non-scrolling overlay, and all options above the fold — 3 event choices at 165px, 4 camp/broker cards at 98px, 5 caravan cards at 77px, and a 4-card reward spread on one row (also verified single-row at 1920x1080). Portrait screenshots at 390x844 and 360x800 were visually inspected: the camp, caravan, and event scenes now fill the frame with the tent/wagon/shrine, horizon, and party all readable. No page errors at either orientation.

Original prompt: The basic is reused In most of the svg interiors. All the interior decorations and placements should be unique. Review all svg interiors and items placed. Also upgrades in interiors should cost materials from other buildings as well to promote cross utilization. Navigation between interiors without needing to go back with an arrow selection system.

- July 25, 2026 Keep interiors stop sharing one furnishing template, expansions cross-utilize workshops, and rooms connect to each other. **Decorations**: `keep.css` drew all 28 placeable furnishings from five `[data-decor-slot="N"]` rules — one pennant, one bowl, one arch, one orb, one lattice — recoloured per room through `--room-accent`, so the Garden's Seed Banners, the Forge's Spark Banner, the Quarry's Mason Banner, and the Generator's Prism Banners were literally the same shape. Every furnishing now has its own `[data-decoration-art="…"]` rule with its own silhouette and its own spot in the room (seedling rack against the woodlot's back wall, blossom arch framing the garden window, ingot mosaic set into the forge floor, aurora ribbon across the fridge ceiling, orrery on a pedestal beside the generator console, echo fountain cut into the quarry floor, crock row and communal bench separated in depth in the kitchen), placed to avoid the fixed furniture already in each room at both phone and desktop stage ratios. The five tool silhouettes were the same rack in seven colours too; each room now owns its mounting and head shape — woodlot peg board with wedge heads, garden tools hanging head-down from a rail, forge hammers on a stone block, fridge chisels with diamond heads in a frost bracket, generator rods glowing in a conduit rail, quarry mauls leaning with no rack at all, kitchen utensils hung from a bar — and the per-room anchor moved out of the mobile media query so each rack keeps its own corner on phones. Recipe copy was boilerplate as well ("A placeable garden furnishing crafted by the sanctuary." on all 28); every tool and decoration now carries its own description. **Cross-utilization**: `addRoomExpansions` priced every tier-2..5 tool and decoration in the room's own material alone. Each room now declares two partner materials (Garden→Frost Crystal/Cut Stone, Forge→Cut Stone/Storm Cell, Fridge→Verdant Fiber/Storm Cell, Generator→Ember Ingot/Frost Crystal, Quarry→Ember Ingot/Verdant Fiber, Kitchen→Verdant Fiber/Cut Stone, Woodlot→Ember Ingot/Cut Stone); `expansionCost` adds the first partner from tier 2 and the second from tier 4, keeping the primary amount unchanged so a room cannot be finished by farming one station. Tier-1 entry items stay single-material so a freshly built workshop still has something to craft. Craft cost chips now carry `is-met`/`is-short` from the held inventory, so a shortfall in a partner material reads without decoding a disabled button. **Navigation**: the interior stage is wrapped in a new `.interior-viewport` that hosts an `interior-nav` overlay — prev/next arrows labelled with the destination room, a clickable dot rail for direct jumps, and ArrowLeft/ArrowRight keys — walking the tour order Hall → Woodlot → Garden → Forge → Fridge → Generator → Quarry → Kitchen → Enclave → Archive with wraparound, skipping any facility that is not built and the Enclave until it is. The arrows live outside the `aria-hidden` stage so they stay reachable, and Grounds still exits. `keep.css` → `?v=21`, `keep.js` → `?v=23`.
- Verification: full `./mvnw test` green (283 tests). Two new `KeepServiceTest` cases — `roomExpansionsCostMaterialsFromPartnerWorkshops` (all 56 tier-2..5 expansions keep their own material, add a partner, reach three materials at tier 4+, and never list a material twice) and `everyExpansionCarriesItsOwnDescription` (no duplicate recipe copy) — plus `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest.keepInteriorsDrawUniqueFurnishingsAndWalkRoomToRoom`, which asserts each of the 28 placeable decorations has a dedicated CSS rule, that the shared `.room-decoration-set [data-decor-slot=` geometry is gone, and that the tour controls exist. `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/keep.js` passes. Headless Chromium against the real `keep.html` at 390x844 and 1440x900 rendered all ten interiors with every decoration placed and all five tools installed; the captures were inspected room by room and three collisions found that way were fixed (garden seed banners overlapping the blossom arch, generator thunder chimes sitting on the prism banners, kitchen crocks running into the communal bench, plus the quarry fountain sitting under the dot rail). A second harness drove the real `keep.js` through `window.__KEEP_TEST_SNAPSHOT__` (a snapshot dumped from `KeepService` with all six facilities at level 2 and the Enclave built): entering the Woodlot from the grounds and pressing Next lands in the Garden with labels "Woodlot"/"Forge" and ten rail dots, Next/Prev walk to Forge and back, ArrowLeft wraps to the Hall, a rail dot jumps straight to the Kitchen and moves the current marker, Grounds still closes the interior, and the Quarry blueprint list renders 8 of 10 recipes with two or three materials (e.g. Counterweight Crane at 24 Cut Stone + 8 Ember Ingot + 5 Verdant Fiber) with the affordability chips applied. No page errors at either viewport. `tests/mobile-overlap` is unaffected — its fixture contains none of the keep-scoped selectors that changed.

Original prompt: In the list separate unread and read lore and messages etc

- July 25, 2026 Keep lists file unread above read: the Chronicle mixed read and unread discoveries in one undivided run, and the notice tray did the same with messages while tracking a single `state.noticeUnread` counter that zeroed the moment the tray opened. `chronicleMarkup` now splits the filtered entries into **Unread** and **Read** groups under a shared `listSection` divider that carries a count; read cards get an `is-read` treatment (receded border, dimmed title) so the unread group reads as the live list. An entry read during a visit holds its place in the Unread group (`state.sessionReadLoreIds`) so the card under the player's finger never jumps as it is marked read, and is re-filed under Read the next time the panel is opened. Messages now carry their own `read` flag: the tray shows **New** and **Earlier** groups, unread rows get a faint gold wash, and the header badge derives from the unread count instead of a counter — messages are marked read when the tray *closes*, which is when the player has actually seen them, keeping the New group and the badge in agreement. The Voices panel picks up the same dividers ("Waiting to speak" / "Spoken with"). `render_game_to_text` reports `noticeCenter.read` alongside `unread`. `keep.css` → `?v=20`, `keep.js` → `?v=22`.

Headless-Chromium run against the real `keep.html` at 390x844 and 1920x1080 via `window.__KEEP_TEST_SNAPSHOT__` (2 unread + 2 read entries, 2 waiting voices, 1 relationship): the Chronicle renders `Unread 2` then `Read 2` in that order with the right cards in each; reading an unread entry drops the heading to `Unread 1` while the card stays in place, and reopening the panel moves it under `Read 3`. Voices shows `Waiting to speak 2` / `Spoken with 1`. Two hall-theme changes drive the tray through `New 1` (badge 1) → closed (badge hidden) → `New 1` + `Earlier 1`. Zero horizontal overflow and no page errors at either viewport; mobile captures of the Chronicle panel and the tray were inspected.

- July 25, 2026 Keep projects no longer re-offer the project a crew is already building: `KeepService.buildOptions` derived availability purely from prerequisites, and a facility's level only rises when its construction completes, so a project under construction stayed in the offer list. Original prompt: Why is the gymstone overlay on the home page way smaller than it appears on dashboard? Fix

- July 23, 2026 Framed "Replace art" illustration no longer collapses smaller on some surfaces: fixed uploaded card art (e.g. Gymstone, cardArtMode REPLACE) rendering far smaller in the home Card View than in the card-dashboard preview. Root cause was the framed art window (`.element-frame .card-art-preview`) being a flex container: the crop rule sized the image with `width: calc(100% * var(--card-art-zoom))` (208%), but as a flex item that width is shrunk back toward the window and clamped to the image's *intrinsic* min-content. While the large source PNG is still downloading (or when a flex engine — notably iOS Safari — doesn't recompute a flex item's min-content after an async image load) the width collapses toward the window and the illustration renders tiny; the dashboard, with the freshly-uploaded art already cached/reflowed, showed the intended size. The 6 `.card-preview-card/.selected-preview-card/.mulligan-showcase .element-frame … .card-art-crop-illustration img` selectors now drive width off the zoomed height (`width: auto` → height × the image's own ratio) with `flex-shrink: 0`, so the art fills the window identically on every surface (home detail, dashboard preview, mulligan/binder showcases) and no longer depends on flex min-content being recomputed mid-load. The loaded appearance is byte-for-byte the same as before (still 141.7% of the window for Gymstone's 2:3 art, i.e. no card re-tuning); only the collapsed/lo­ading state is fixed. `style.css` cache pins advanced to `?v=214` on `home.html`, `card-dashboard.html`, and `play.html`.
- Verification: CSS brace balance intact. Faithful headless-Chromium reproduction at 390x844 (real `home.html`, live `/api/game/options` stubbed with the production Gymstone entry, the real Firebase art routed locally) and 1100x1400 (real `card-dashboard.html`, live `/api/cards/editor` stubbed): before the fix, home rendered the art at ratio 0.580 while the source PNG was loading and 0.822 once loaded, while the dashboard sat at 0.822 — reproducing "smaller on home". After the fix both surfaces render at 0.822 (identical), and a delayed-load probe confirmed the art no longer collapses to a wrong small size mid-download (it stays absent until the image loads, then paints at full size). Loaded mobile screenshots of both surfaces were visually inspected and match (whole creature framed by its aura, no re-crop).

Original prompt: On desktop I am logged in but everytime I select the keep it does not register me as logged in. If you log in from the keep page instead of going back to home just create a login pop in keep and continue from there.

- July 21, 2026 Keep sign-in persistence + in-page login: fixed the My Keep gate appearing for keepers who are already signed in on desktop. Root cause: `keep.js` `fetchJson` attached the localStorage `sieglingsAuthToken` as an `Authorization: Bearer` header whenever any token was present, which pre-empted the httpOnly `sgl_session` cookie (the server's `SessionCookieAuthFilter` only bridges the cookie when no Authorization header is sent). A stale/expired legacy token therefore forced a 401 even though the cookie was valid. `keep.js` now mirrors `home.js`: it prefers the cookie whenever the readable `sgl_auth` companion cookie is present and the page isn't a standalone Web App, migrates a lingering real token to the `cookie` sentinel on load, and only sends a Bearer header when the cookie genuinely can't carry the session. Separately, the gate's "Sign in" no longer navigates to `/login` (which returned keepers to `/home`); it opens an in-page login/register modal, and on success the gate and modal dismiss and the keep snapshot reloads in place so rebuilding continues on My Keep. Cache pins advanced to `keep.css?v=15` and `keep.js?v=17`.
- Verification: `node --check src/main/resources/static/js/keep.js` passes. A focused Playwright run at `390x844` and `1920x1080` (with mocked `/api/keep` and `/api/auth/login`) confirmed: the gate shows while unauthenticated; "Sign in" opens the in-page modal without leaving `/keep.html`; submitting credentials closes the gate/modal, re-fetches `/api/keep`, and stays on the keep; and after the readable auth cookie is set the keep re-fetch sends no Bearer header (cookie auth), the exact regression fixed. All assertions passed at both viewports.

- July 21, 2026 holographic full-card art sizing (no more standard frame peeking): dedicated holographic full-card art on the Battle Table board tiles and the mobile "Card Preview" panel now fills its card box completely instead of being letterboxed. Both surfaces render the holo face through `renderCardArt` → `.card-art.game-holographic-full-card-art` (an absolute `inset:0` image layered over the standard framed card), and the image used `object-fit: contain`. Because the holo source is ~639x919 (aspect 0.695) while the card shell is 5/7 (0.714), `contain` shrank the art and exposed the painted element frame behind it as a ~2–4px sliver on the left/right edges — reading as a second, smaller card laid on top of the standard one. The image now uses `object-fit: fill` so it stretches to the box exactly like the painted element frame it replaces (which fills via `background-size: 100% 100%`); the ~2.7% horizontal stretch matches the frame's own, keeping the baked artwork registered with the live name/HP/notch overlays that are positioned as percentages of that same box. Marked the width/height/`object-fit` overrides `!important` to beat the more-specific element-frame art-window rule (`.arena-board-card.element-frame .card-art-hand img`, auto width + `contain`) that was re-letterboxing board tiles, mirroring the container rule directly above. The binder/mulligan/hand-tray full-art path (holographic.css `.binder-full-card-art-image`) is unchanged — it keeps `contain` over a dark canvas, so it needs no fill. Bumped `style.css` to v213 across play/home/card-dashboard.
- Verification: focused `GameJavaScriptRegressionTest` passes 20/20 (its holo-art guard was retargeted from the now-misleading generic `object-fit: contain;` substring to the scoped `.hand-card .card-art.game-holographic-full-card-art img` rule with `object-fit: fill !important;`). A headless-Chromium harness at 390x844 rendered the real `style.css` against faithful board and mobile-preview DOM using a 638x919 stand-in image: before, the preview image measured a 2.1px letterbox gap per side (fire frame showing behind); after, both the board tile and preview image report computed `object-fit: fill` and fill their card box exactly (img box == card box, 0 gap), with the standard frame fully covered. Before/after screenshots were visually inspected.

- July 21, 2026 starter deck registration / gacha unbroken: new-player starter pack selection could persist the pack grant and then fail the follow-on profile-defaults save with `Choose a Siegeling you own.` because the server picked the first catalog Siegeling of the element rather than one from the pack grant. The client treated that as a hard open failure (and a 15s timeout could strand the same half-complete account), so registration looked broken even though `starterChosen` was already true. `PlayerProgressionController` now picks an owned starter favorite Siegeling, treats profile defaults as best-effort after the grant, and still returns progression/gacha payload on profile hiccups. `home.js` raises starter opens to a 60s timeout, recovers from `/api/player/progression` when the POST errors after a successful grant, and routes starter gacha Done to Home + the onboarding tour. Cache bust: `home.js?v=108`.

- July 20, 2026 My Keep round 2 — interiors, 2.5D building art, orientation fix, first-visit tutorial, keep achievements: (1) **Orientation/header bug**: iOS Safari can leave the document scrolled after a portrait↔landscape rotation even with `overflow:hidden`, which pushed the fixed header (and its only exit link) off-screen with no way back. `.keep-app` is now `position:fixed; inset:0` so the shell is anchored to the viewport, `html/body` gained `overscroll-behavior:none`, and `keep.js` snaps `window.scrollTo(0,0)` on `resize`/`orientationchange`/`scroll` (with delayed retries after rotation, matching iOS's late scroll application). (2) **Building interiors**: tapping any building now opens a full-screen interior (`#keepInterior`, grid of title bar / CSS-art room stage / action column) instead of the side panel — Covenant Hall (banners, hearth, charter, long table), Woodlot lodge (plank walls, grove window, workbench, log stacks, invited-resident avatar), and the Archive with ruined (cracks, fallen beam, collapsed shelf spilling books, rubble, buried glowing relic) vs restored (bookshelves, purple arched window, reading desk, lamp, memorabilia plinth honoring `displayed`) variants keyed off `data-archive-restored`. The old building side-panel markup was refactored into shared `buildingHeading`/`buildingMarkup` builders that now render into the interior's action area (Collect/Invite/Projects/Chronicle all work inside), Escape order is tutorial → dialogue → panel → interior, and the mobile layout stacks stage over a scrollable action sheet while desktop/landscape use a right column. (3) **Paper-Mario fix**: the three exterior buildings were rebuilt as inline 2.5D SVGs — front facade + darker receding side face + dual roof planes + cylindrical tower shading + contact shadows — replacing the flat front-facing CSS shapes; state hooks preserved (`.ar-ruined`/`.ar-restored` groups, `.wl-lvl2` lean-to at Woodlot level 2, scaffold overlays now present on *both* woodlot and archive during construction). (4) **Tutorial**: a 4-step first-visit overlay (`#keepTutorial`, `localStorage.sieglingsKeepTutorialSeen`) explains the wounded ground premise, Woodlot production + Collect, Projects/offline construction, and interiors/Chronicle/Voices; a new header "?" button replays it anytime. (5) **Achievements & badges**: `PlayerProgressionEntity`/`Store`/serialize gained lifetime `keepFounded/keepTimberCollected/keepProjectsCompleted/keepLoreRead/keepConversationsCompleted`, recorded best-effort from `KeepService` (founding on first load, collect grants, construction completions in both snapshot and mutate paths, first-reads, conversations — idempotent replays and re-reads do not double-count); 10 new badges in a new `keep` category resolve in both `achievements.js` (165→175) and the server `AchievementEvaluationService`, each with a matching player title appended to `achievement-titles.json`. Cache busts: `keep.css?v=2`, `keep.js?v=3` (keep.html), `achievements.js?v=7` (home.html).
- Verification: full Maven suite green (248 tests) including a new `KeepServiceTest.keepActionsRecordLifetimeProgressionStats` covering founding, collect (with request-id replay), project completion, first-read-only lore counting, and conversation stats. `node --check` clean on `keep.js` and `achievements.js`; a Node harness confirmed the keep category holds 10 badges with correct thresholds and that a sample progression unlocks exactly the expected five. A headless-Chromium harness (mock `__KEEP_TEST_SNAPSHOT__`) passed 37/37 checks across 390x844 portrait, 844x390 landscape, and 1920x1080: tutorial shows once/replays/persists its flag, all three interiors open/exit (button + Escape), ruined vs restored archive art and interiors swap on `visualState`, the Woodlot level-2 extension appears, the landscape header/brand exit and dock stay on-screen, a simulated stray 140px scroll snaps back to 0 with the header still at y=0, and desktop has zero horizontal overflow. Portrait/landscape/desktop screenshots of the scene, tutorial, and all interiors were visually inspected.

Added an HP stat tile alongside Hand and Deck in both the enemy and player panels of `play.html`, wrapping the three tiles in a new full-width `.m-count-row` (a `repeat(3, minmax(0,1fr))` grid inside the existing `.stat-sheet-content` grid) so they read as equal tiles at every breakpoint — the same markup drives the portrait bottom-sheet and the landscape top-anchored flyout, so both orientations get HP for free. - July 20, 2026 achievements, weekly/lifetime missions, daily login reward, siege badges, and featured badge selection: expanded the rewards loop across missions, achievements, and the profile badge case. (1) Missions now span three scopes — a new `MissionPeriod` (DAILY/WEEKLY/LIFETIME) drives `DailyMissionDefinition`/`DailyMissionCatalog` (6 weekly, 6 lifetime templates added alongside the existing daily set), and `DailyMissionProgressEntity`/`DailyMissionProgressStore` gained weekly + lifetime counter maps, per-scope claimed-id lists, an ISO-week key, and daily-login fields. `DailyMissionService` increments every scope on each event (`recordMatch`/`recordPackOpened`/new `recordSiege`), resets daily/weekly on their boundaries, keeps lifetime cumulative, serializes `daily`/`weekly`/`lifetime`/`login` blocks (daily `missions`/`featured` kept at top level for backward compat), and claims by looking a mission up in any period. (2) A flat **100-coin daily login reward** (`claimLoginReward` + `POST /api/missions/claim-login`) grants once per calendar day and tracks a consecutive-day streak. (3) **Siege-mode progression**: `PlayerProgressionEntity`/`Store`/serialize gained lifetime `siegeRuns/siegeWins/siegeBossKills/siegeNodesCleared/siegeBestScore`, incremented in `SiegeService.grantEndRewards` (which also feeds `recordSiege`); 12 new siege achievements (new `siege` category) resolve in both `achievements.js` and the server `AchievementEvaluationService`, each with a matching player title in `achievement-titles.json`. (4) **Featured badge selection**: `ProfileSettingsEntity`/`Store`/`Service` persist up to six `featuredBadgeIds`; `achievements.js` `evaluateAll` now resolves the six profile badge-case slots from the player's picks (padded with the default showcase set). Frontend `home.js` adds Daily/Weekly/Lifetime mission tabs, the login-reward tile, and a badge-case customization panel on the achievements overview (pick unlocked badges, ordered, saved via `/api/profile/settings`). New CSS for `.mission-tabs`, `.login-reward-row`, and `.featured-*`. Bumped `achievements.js?v=6`, `home.js?v=107`, `home.css?v=108`.
- Verification: full Maven suite green (239 tests) including new `DailyMissionServiceTest` cases (siege counters across all scopes, login reward once-per-day + streak, weekly mission claim). `node --check` clean on `home.js` and `achievements.js`. A Node harness loaded `achievements.js` and confirmed the siege category holds 12 badges with correct unlock thresholds and that custom featured ids sort ahead of defaults to exactly six slots (total badge count 153→165). A headless-Chromium render of the new mission panel + featured selector at 390x844 and 1920x1080 showed correct tabs (Weekly active), the login tile with streak/Claim, progress rows, and the numbered badge-pick grid with Save/Cancel, with zero horizontal overflow at both viewports; the mobile capture was visually inspected.

- July 19, 2026 "No active game" mid-match fix — Cloud Run single-instance pin + client recovery: root-caused the intermittent "No active game. Start a new game first." toast that fires mid-match (e.g. on the mulligan Keep Hand / Redraw). Solo match state lives only in one JVM's memory (`GameService.soloGames`, mirroring `MultiplayerService.rooms`), but the Cloud Run deploy used default autoscaling — requests load-balance across multiple instances that also scale to zero between calls, so a game started on instance A vanishes when the next action lands on instance B (or A after a cold start), and `requireSoloState` throws. Pinned `sieglings-tcg-api` to a single always-warm instance (`--min-instances 1 --max-instances 1` in `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`) so every request hits the same in-memory store; min=1 also removes the 10-30s cold start on `/api/game/new`. This matches the architecture the match layer already assumes. Added a client-side safety net in `game.js` `api()`: when a solo (non-multiplayer) action returns a `No active game` error, the stale `soloToken` is cleared and the entry overlays re-sync, so a session lost across a redeploy or the 6h TTL returns the player to the start screen instead of re-toasting a dead session. Bumped `game.js?v=204`→`205` in play/home/card-dashboard.
- Verification: `node --check` passes on `game.js`; `deploy.yml` validated as well-formed YAML (`yaml.safe_load`); confirmed `syncEntryOverlays` and `setSoloSessionToken` exist and the new branch is scoped to solo actions (guarded by `!multiplayerSession && soloSessionToken`) so multiplayer error handling is unchanged. Traced the error string to a single source (`GameController.requireSoloState`, line 347) so the client's `startsWith('No active game')` match is unambiguous.

- July 11, 2026 Siege modern full-bleed battle + hand-clip bugfix (round 9): modernized the phone-landscape Siege battle into a full-bleed arena with floating overlay chrome, and fixed the hand cards being clipped at the bottom in BOTH orientations. Bugfix (JS `adventure.js` + CSS): the fan arc used to push edge cards DOWN (`--fan-y` positive) past the `.hand-fan` `overflow-y:hidden` clip line, cutting off card bottoms; `layoutHandFan` now lifts edge cards UP (`--fan-y` negated, `HAND_FAN_MAX_LIFT` 30→26) so the lowest card always hugs the flex-end baseline and nothing dips past the bottom — this cures the clip everywhere. Portrait additionally under-reserved space for the fixed `.battle-hud` (min-height 58 but real height ~72 incl. the End Turn pill), so the hud overlapped the lowest cards; bumped `--battle-mobile-hud-h` 58→72 and grew the portrait `.hand-fan` to `min-height:158px` with room for the upward arc. Portrait layout is otherwise unchanged. Landscape redesign (CSS, the `orientation:landscape and max-width:979 and max-height:600` block): the top bar drops to `position:absolute` so `#battleScreen` fills the full viewport height and `.battle-stage` goes `position:absolute; inset:0` (no border/radius) as an edge-to-edge arena; allies pack the FAR LEFT and enemies the FAR RIGHT (`.ally-line align-items/align-content:flex-start`, `.foe-line:flex-end`, lines `flex:0 1 auto`) with a wide empty center gap; sprites grow to 92px. All chrome floats as absolutely-positioned overlays anchored to `#battleScreen` (position:relative — NOT fixed, dodging the iOS fixed-in-filtered-ancestor trap): knight/life HUD (with Revive/Potion item chips) + condensed speed strip top-left, hint/turn banner top-center, ledger toggle top-right below Abandon Run, hand fan bottom-center (tall enough for the arc), AP pips + deck/discard chips as bottom-left panels, and a big rounded End Turn (+ultimate) bottom-right; the hud bar is `pointer-events:none` (children re-enabled) so its empty center passes drags to the fan. Drag focus (JS + CSS, global): dragging a targeted card now adds `siege-drag-active` to `<body>`, dimming the arena via a `.battle-stage::after` scrim (pointer-events:none, so `elementFromPoint` drops still land) while the existing top-layer SVG trajectory arc + pulsing target ring and the `.sprite.drop-hover` lock-on lift/glow read as the focus; `user-select:none` on the battle screen stops the drag rubber-band-selecting text. Server untouched. Cache bust: `adventure.css?v=34`, `adventure.js?v=37` (adventure.html; no other page includes them).

Landscape 956x440 AND 844x390: `.battle-stage` spans 98%×100% of the viewport (full-bleed); every `.ally-line .sprite` centerX (64) sits in the left third and every `.foe-line .sprite` centerX (892 / 780) in the right third; knight HUD top-left, AP pips left side, End Turn bottom-right quadrant 44px tall, deck chips visible, hint top-center; every hand card fully in-viewport with 0px² overlap against the real AP/End-Turn widgets; a REAL pointer drag of a targeted card engaged the drag-focus (`siege-drag-active` on body, `.sprite.drop-hover` on the target, arc SVG `is-active` with paths), and the drop was ACCEPTED by the server (hand 4→3) with the arc gone afterward; the ledger opened and the Revive/Potion item chips are top-left and clickable; no page errors. - July 11, 2026 Siege phone-landscape horizontal map + facing-columns battle (round 8): building on round 7's landscape shell, the Siege map now flows LEFT→RIGHT and the battle faces the player's team LEFT vs enemies RIGHT, phone-landscape only. Map (JS, `adventure.js` `renderMap()`): a new `isPhoneLandscape()` guard (same `orientation:landscape and max-width:979 and max-height:600` breakpoint as the CSS block) transposes the SVG DAG — depth (`row`) maps to X with row 0 at the far LEFT and the boss at the far RIGHT, lanes (`col`) spread down Y centered; edges bend through `midX` instead of `midY`; a tighter lane axis (`landPad:40`, `landLaneGap:60`) keeps the common 3-4 lane maps inside the short viewport height; auto-scroll targets `scrollLeft` (60% lead, far-left when no current node). A debounced `resize`/`orientationchange` listener (`onMapOrientationFlip`, registered in `boot`) re-renders when the axis flips while on `mapScreen`, since the SVG geometry is baked at render time; `mapLayoutLand` remembers the last-drawn axis. Battle (CSS, `adventure.css`, inside the round-7 landscape block): `.battle-stage` becomes `flex-direction:row-reverse` so `.ally-line` (DOM-second) sits LEFT and `.foe-line` (DOM-first) sits RIGHT with a center gap (the clash/impact-FX lane); each line is a `flex-direction:column; flex-wrap:wrap` formation with `align-content`/`align-items` set so each side's front rank hugs the center — a 5-6 unit warband forms a second inward rank instead of shrinking. `#mapScroll` gets `overflow-x:auto; overflow-y:auto` (auto not hidden as a safety net so the widest 5-lane maps stay reachable) and `#mapSvg` centers vertically (`margin:auto 0`). Sprite floaters/intents are sprite-anchored and unchanged; the pointer drag (`elementFromPoint`) is layout-independent. Portrait (`max-height>600`) and desktop never match either guard, so both are byte-for-byte unchanged. Cache bust: `adventure.css?v=33`, `adventure.js?v=36` (adventure.html; no other page includes them).

- July 9, 2026 landscape rounds 5-6 (owner feedback + device-only regression): the fixed bottom action HUD vanished on real iPhones while desktop emulation rendered it — iOS WebKit derives position:fixed containing blocks from more ancestor effects than Chromium, trapping the strip inside the overflow-hidden rail — so `game.js` now reparents `.action-bar-aux` to `<body>` in phone landscape (`syncLandscapeAuxHud`, called from `updateResponsiveLayoutVars`) and restores it into `#actionBar` in portrait.
