The landscape mulligan pins to the viewport top with a height budget (kicker/copy clamp, card cap `calc((100dvh - 228px) * 5/7)`) so the OPENING HAND header no longer clips and the box never scrolls; the loadout accept button is capped at 300px so the summary stays readable. The `/play` welcome hero became a single readable column with the mode buttons in a wrapping row (Opus subagent's task A). `home.css` gained a 901-979px landscape block wrapping the hub nav rows so Filters/Play/Sign-in/Friends stay on-screen (the non-wrapping HUD row overflowed and pushed the Filters button past the right edge), with the filter tray bounded to `100dvh`. `card-binder-visual.js` gained `scheduleDescriptionFit` — a proportional font stepper (x0.93 per step, floor 4.8px, tightened line-height) that makes every `.binder-card-description` fit fully inside its painted panel, hooked into all five `home.js` render sites plus a debounced resize listener. - July 9, 2026 phone landscape battle-table repair: rebuilt the landscape (`max-width: 979px`) hand selector after it stretched a lone card to the full sidebar height and pushed the action bar off-screen. The old JS fit (`--hand-card-height` = tray height / card count) measured content-sized boxes, so its own output fed the next measurement; `syncDesktopHandSelectorCardScale` in `js/game.js` now skips sizing entirely when the hand container computes as `display: grid` (and the remaining vertical path is capped at the card's 5:7 aspect as insurance). `css/style.css` lays the landscape hand out as a CSS-owned two-column scrollable grid of aspect-true cards, hides the decorative desktop-menu header, floors the inspector at 96px with `flex: 1 1 0%` (basis `auto` let a tall preview squeeze the dock below its floor and clip the action bar), and lets the bottom dock flex-shrink so Knight/Draw/aux buttons always stay on-screen — including battle phase, via `body.battle-inline-dock` counter-overrides scoped to the landscape block. Landscape boards also grew ~2.4x: a `.battlefield .board-grid` rule with wide (1.92) cells sized from arena height (`min(98%, calc(96cqh - 84px), 520px)`) now outranks the portrait container-query rule that had shrunk them to ~125px, and the old `max-height: 430px` board block was folded into it so 440px-tall phones (iPhone Pro Max landscape) are covered. Cache busting advanced to `style.css?v=188` and `game.js?v=190` across `play.html`, `home.html`, and `card-dashboard.html`.

- April 4, 2026 gameplay/UI fix pass: raised the player/enemy max HP cap down to 50, reversed game log ordering so newest entries render first, allowed evolution placements even when the board already has 5 Sieglings, and added a soft opening-draw bias so early hands are more likely to include basic Sieglings plus at least one cheap support card.
- Fixed speed-zero recovery rules in the backend. `SPEED_ZERO` now sets current speed to 0, later speed boosts can lift that card back above 0, and the new `EffectServiceTest.speedBoostCanRestoreMovementAfterSpeedZero()` passes under `.\mvnw.cmd -q test`.

Target-based hand action cards now surface the same "no targets available" lock reason.
- Desktop preview sizing follow-up: the right-dock card preview no longer overflows its section because the preview section now flexes correctly with the title above it. The preview card/art/text were scaled down slightly so the full card summary stays in bounds without needing a visible scrollbar.

- April 3, 2026 desktop hover cleanup: restricted the floating focused-card preview over the enemy half to the mobile layout only. Wide desktop now keeps the existing hand hover/tooltip behavior without the oversized mobile-style preview covering the enemy board, and window resize now re-syncs the floating preview plus the lifted hand card layer immediately.
- Added and iterated on turn flow, placement, energy, and battle rules across the Spring Boot web game.
- Latest change: moved board notch dots to the actual card boundary so edge/corner links render on the rectangle perimeter instead of floating inward.
- April 1, 2026 popup preview follow-up: restored the floating card preview as a full rendered card instead of the old text summary box. Locked hand cards can now still be selected for inspection, the selected-card drawer reuses the same full-card renderer, and both views show notch dots on the real card perimeter. Local Playwright screenshots in `output/web-game/popup-restored-check` confirmed the popup and drawer on both mobile and desktop after restarting Spring Boot from source.
- Added Earth and Wind as first-class elements using the Roblox reference project's names/colors/archetypes (`CreatureData.lua` / `ArenaSystem.lua`), expanded energy tracking/UI to four elements, and switched the starter matchup to Fire/Earth vs Water/Wind.
- Raised Siegling HP floors so all creature cards now start at 10+ HP via the scaled health helper in `src/main/java/com/sieglings/service/CardDefinitionService.java`.
- Reworked internal mixed-element links into combo points. Same-element links are now the only source of normal internal energy; mixed internal points produce combo records like `EARTH+FIRE`, including 3-way and 4-way crossroads.
- Added combo-spell requirements in `SpellCard` plus starter-deck combo spells such as `Emberroot Burst` and `Storm Current`. Live API now exposes combo counts and combo point signatures to the UI.
- Replaced sidebar energy stats with a visual Energy Bucket made of per-energy circles plus combo pie tokens, and added an element color key to the right panel.
- Added a pre-match loadout flow with 8 preset decks (4 single-element, 4 mixed including one four-element deck) and SiegeKnight selection before the game starts. Backend exposes `/api/game/options`, and `new game` now accepts `deckId` + `trainerId`.
- Added trap cards as a full card type. Traps now serialize into the hand/UI, use opponent energy-bucket requirements instead of your own energy, and the AI can spring them during setup. Shared action-card targeting now reads `targetType` metadata instead of guessing from description text.
- Added a one-Siegling-per-turn placement cap for both player and AI. Game state now tracks per-turn placements, the backend blocks second placements with a clear log message, and the frontend stops highlighting placement cells after your first placement resolves.
- Added stronger trap support in deck construction by giving trap cards a light playset presence instead of singleton odds, so elemental and mixed decks draw them more consistently.
- Added elemental weakness bonus damage for Siegling attacks using the Roblox reference chart subset now active in the TCG. Current live matchups: Water gets +1 vs Fire/Earth, Earth gets +1 vs Wind, and Wind gets +1 vs Fire. Weakness bonus is applied only to source-based Siegling damage, not generic spells/traps.
- Adjusted board card layout so creature names sit centered inside the cell while HP/stats/statuses are anchored above the bottom edge, reducing overlap with lower notch points and the stat strip.
- Added selected common battle fallbacks: a few commons now get zero-cost weak poke attacks or zero-cost self-move abilities that reposition them to an open linked point during battle. Verified live that `Flame Sprite` exposes `Flame Sprite Drift` as a 0-energy `SELF` action.
- Added Roblox-backed evolution placement rules for the current TCG lines: `Cacty -> Jacked'ty`, `Pylme -> Bonoblade`, `Breezee -> Gagglestand`, and `Pursula -> Purseus`. Evolution cards now target the occupied precursor cell, and the evolved board instance merges inherited notches with the new card's added links. Live API check confirmed `Player evolves Cacty into Jacked'ty...` and the merged notch set `TOP,RIGHT,BOTTOM,LEFT`.
- Added Shadow and Electric as live TCG elements while keeping Water active. The backend now tracks six elemental energies, the deck builder/startup picker includes `deck_shadow` and `deck_electric`, and the generated catalogs now include Roblox-backed Shadow and Lightning/Electric rosters plus 30 spells / 2 traps per new element.
- Added `Night Regent` and `Volt Shepherd` as SiegeKnight options, updated the UI color system / energy bucket / element key for Shadow and Electric, and fixed `GameService`'s preset fallback so the app no longer depends on a hardcoded deck list index.
- Latest verification: `-DskipTests compile` passes, the app is running again on port 8080 from `spring-boot-16.log`, `/api/game/options` now returns `10` decks and `8` trainers, and a live `deck_shadow` start produced a Shadow opening hand with `shadowEnergy` and `electricEnergy` fields present in the serialized player state.
- Node/npx are not installed in this environment, so the Playwright loop from the web-game skill could not be run locally. Verification is currently manual via the running Spring Boot app at http://localhost:8080.

- If further visual tuning is needed, continue adjusting `.board-notches` inset and the `.notch-*` anchor positions in `src/main/resources/static/css/style.css`.

The left panel now renders as concise enemy/player/action summary blocks, the right panel now has mobile-only tabs for `Battle / Card / Log / Key`, the board compresses to a smaller responsive footprint, and the hand switches to a dense 3-column card grid with truncated copy instead of a long scrolling strip.

That now only refreshes the room status + summary, which stops the chooser from looking like it is constantly reloading.

Local Spring Boot was restarted from `spring-boot-23.log` and now serves the same updated markup on `http://localhost:8080`.
- March 29, 2026 deployment fix: the hosted app could land on the empty board shell when the initial `/api/game/options` request stalled, because `loadGameOptions()` only opened the loadout overlay after the request completed. `src/main/resources/static/js/game.js` now opens the loadout overlay immediately on startup and wraps JSON fetches in a 10-second timeout so stalled backend requests turn into a retryable error instead of a blank board.

Diagonal outward notches no longer generate energy.

- March 29, 2026 opening flow/account pass: added a database-backed account system using Spring Data JPA + file-backed H2 (`data/sieglings.mv.db`). New endpoints now support email register/login/logout plus `/api/auth/me`, saved deck CRUD, and recent match history reads. Passwords are hashed with BCrypt and auth uses bearer tokens stored in the database.
- Added opening-hand mulligans to game startup. New matches now begin in `MULLIGAN`, solo AI resolves its mulligan automatically, multiplayer waits for both players, and `POST /api/game/mulligan` advances the game into the first draw phase once both sides lock their hands.
- Added a new welcome screen in `src/main/resources/static/index.html` and `src/main/resources/static/js/game.js` with a 4-slide visual tutorial covering placement, link formation, socket energy, and setup-vs-battle energy pacing. The loadout screen now sits behind that welcome flow instead of appearing immediately on startup.
- Added account-aware saved deck UI inside the loadout overlay. Logged-in players can save the current preset or custom builder loadout with a name, reload saved lists later, and start games with that saved loadout label so future match history can show the deck name.
- Added a mulligan overlay/UI flow in `src/main/resources/static/index.html`, `src/main/resources/static/js/game.js`, and `src/main/resources/static/css/style.css` so the player sees the current opening hand and can choose `Keep Hand` or `Mulligan` once.

- March 30, 2026 backend socket regression pass: `EnergyService` now models outside energy through explicit perimeter socket keys before tallying totals, so the backend matches the UI's actual socket-point model instead of counting raw off-board notches. Added `src/test/java/com/sieglings/service/EnergyServiceTest.java` coverage for the reported enemy front-left `Clawkid` case and for a diagonal-only enemy front-left notch, both confirming that diagonals do not create extra socket energy.
- March 30, 2026 compile cleanup: added `spring-boot-starter-test` to `pom.xml` and fixed enum switch expressions that broke full compile/test runs after the extra placeholder elements were added in `CardDefinitionService`, `EffectService`, `GeneratedSpellCatalog`, and `GeneratedCreatureCatalog`.
- Latest verification: `.\mvnw.cmd -q -DskipTests compile` passes and `.\mvnw.cmd -q test` passes from `A:\New folder\OneDrive\Desktop\Sieglings\sieglings-tcg`.
- March 30, 2026 disconnected-energy fix: `EnergyService` now analyzes only the active connected foundation network by reusing `PlacementService.getFoundationSieglings(...)`. This means movement abilities that break reciprocal links now immediately drop the energy gained from those broken links after battle-phase recalculation.
- Added a move regression in `src/test/java/com/sieglings/service/EnergyServiceTest.java` that moves a linked Siegling to an isolated point and verifies the broken internal energy disappears after `recalculateEnergy`.
- Latest verification: `.\mvnw.cmd -q test` passes after the disconnected-energy fix.
- March 30, 2026 preset deck rebalance: `CardDefinitionService` now builds preset decks with an exact `20 Sieglings / 10 spells / 10 traps` structure instead of dumping the whole element pool. Siegling packages are assembled from full evolution lines only, never exceed 3 copies per card, and distribute the 20-creature target across the deck's included elements.
- Spell and trap packages now use fixed-size repeated bundles that prefer lower-cost, lower-combo, on-theme options first, which makes the preset lists less clogged with expensive setup cards.
- Added `src/test/java/com/sieglings/service/CardDefinitionServiceTest.java` to verify every preset deck option builds to 40 cards total, contains the exact `20 / 10 / 10` split, respects the 3-copy limit, and includes full evolution trees whenever any member of a Siegling line appears.
- Latest verification: `.\mvnw.cmd -q -DskipTests compile` passes and `.\mvnw.cmd -q test` passes after the preset deck rebalance.
- March 30, 2026 stat-system simplification: Sieglings now expose only `Health`, `Speed`, notches, and abilities. The old printed `Attack` and `Defense` stats were removed from `SieglingCard`, `CardInstance`, API serialization, and the frontend card/board/tooltips.
- Damage now comes from battle abilities and printed attack abilities instead of a permanent attack stat. Temporary offense effects were reshaped into `damage_boost`, while old defense-style buffs were converted into `health_boost` so durability is expressed through Health instead of hidden damage reduction.
- Trainer rosters, generated creatures, and generated spells were updated so their text/effects now reference attack-damage boosts, health boosts, healing, speed, freeze, and destroy effects instead of `Attack/Defense` stat buffs.
- Latest verification: `.\mvnw.cmd -q test` passes after the stat-system simplification. A direct `node --check` pass could not be run in this shell because `node` is not installed.
- March 30, 2026 card-render cleanup: the live `/js/game.js` bundle now formats Siegling stat lines defensively so missing values no longer print `undefined` chips on hand, board, preview, selected-card, or tooltip surfaces. Board stat badges and HP bars now render only from numeric values.
- `Staticap.png` was confirmed to exist and load from `/images/cards/Staticap.png`, but the file is a full-card scan rather than a cropped illustration. The renderer now treats that asset as an illustration-crop source, zooming/focusing the artwork pane so the old embedded frame/stat area does not dominate the card face.
- Cache-bust versions were advanced in `src/main/resources/static/index.html` to `style.css?v=16` and `game.js?v=15`.
- Latest verification: `.\mvnw.cmd -q -DskipTests compile` passes after the card-render cleanup. Playwright/browser screenshot validation is still blocked in this shell because the packaged `node` binary is not present.
- March 31, 2026 placement-anchor update: non-evolution Sieglings can now be placed using either a reciprocal creature-notch link or a compatible perimeter socket on that empty cell. Left-edge cells accept `LEFT`, right-edge cells accept `RIGHT`, and the board's outer row accepts `BOTTOM` for the player side or `TOP` for the enemy side.
- `PlacementService.getFoundationSieglings(...)` now treats externally anchored edge cards as valid foundation seeds too, so cards placed via sockets remain live for energy generation and future expansion instead of becoming dead isolated pieces.
- Frontend placement previews in `src/main/resources/static/js/game.js` now mirror that rule by allowing highlighted legal cells when the selected Siegling can anchor to an open external socket, not only when it can link to a neighboring creature.
- Added `src/test/java/com/sieglings/service/PlacementServiceTest.java` coverage for socket-only placement legality and for externally anchored cards remaining in the foundation network. Updated `EnergyServiceTest` so the broken-link movement regression still checks a truly unanchored destination under the new rule.
- Latest verification: `.\mvnw.cmd -q test` passes after the placement-anchor update.
- March 31, 2026 manual card-tuning system: Siegling stats, notches, cost, evolution data, and abilities can now be overridden through `src/main/resources/cards/siegling-overrides.json` without hand-rewriting the generated roster. `GeneratedCreatureCatalog` still supplies the baseline cards, and `ManualSieglingCatalog` merges any listed overrides on top.
- Added `CARD_TUNING.md` with the editable JSON shape and an example override, plus `ManualSieglingCatalogTest` coverage proving a hand-authored definition can replace generated rarity, health, speed, notches, cost, and ability fields.
- March 31, 2026 ability effect-key registry: added `AbilityEffectKeys` plus `ABILITY_EFFECT_KEYS.md` so ability definitions and manual override JSON have a single reference for supported effect strings instead of relying on scattered magic values.
- Added `connected_allies_health_boost`, a new source-based effect that gives max Health to allied Sieglings connected to the source card's live reciprocal-link network. `EffectService` now resolves it from the source card, `BattleService` preserves sensible self-targeting when passive creature abilities are converted into active battle abilities, and `EffectServiceTest` covers the linked-vs-isolated ally case.
- March 31, 2026 connected-effect expansion: added `connected_allies_damage_boost` alongside the connected-health effect so link-network abilities can now buff either attack damage or max Health on connected allies only. `ABILITY_EFFECT_KEYS.md` documents both, and `EffectServiceTest` now covers both families.
- March 31, 2026 connected-effect expansion follow-up: added `connected_allies_speed_boost` so connected-network abilities can now buff attack damage, max Health, or Speed on linked allies only. `BattleService` now treats all three connected buff keys as source-based self-target abilities, and `EffectServiceTest` covers the speed variant too.
- March 31, 2026 override JSON ordering pass: reordered `src/main/resources/cards/siegling-overrides.json` by element, then grouped full evolution lines together within each element, with standalone creatures listed after the evo groups for easier manual tuning.
- March 31, 2026 multi-ability override support: Sieglings can now carry an explicit `abilities` array in `siegling-overrides.json`, with each ability defining its own `requiredElement` and `requiredEnergy`. `BattleService` treats that array as the card's explicit battle loadout, preserving per-ability costs instead of overwriting them with rarity defaults, and the frontend card views now render ability lists instead of assuming a single printed effect.

- July 20, 2026 My Keep elemental-economy expansion: extended the server-authoritative Keep state and Firestore persistence with shared Elemental Essence, a two-level Covenant Storehouse, four two-level production facilities (Covenant Garden, Accord Forge, Frost Fridge, Elemental Generator), independent facility storage/remainders/accrual clocks, and one resident slot per working station. Storehouse levels raise timber/essence inventory caps and multiply every workstation's offline storage; the four affinity groups cover all elemental families, matching residents give +20% facility output, Neutral gives +5%, and the Woodlot retains its +15% woodland bonus. The restoration chain now runs Archive → Woodlot II → Storehouse → Garden → Forge/Fridge → Generator, followed by level-two masterworks, using timber and essence costs with long offline timers.
- Rewards/lore/return loop: added one-time Keep milestones plus a seven-day sanctuary tribute that grant Siegecoins and Remnants through progression-side persisted claim ids. Five facility-earned lore drops and a Quartermaster Sera conversation connect storage, gardening, forging, preservation, and the shared-current Generator to the First Covenant's consent/stewardship themes. Returning after at least five minutes now materializes production and completed construction into a “While you were away” report covering timber/essence gains, finished projects, lore found, and capped stations.
- UI: the grounds now show an animated code-native Elemental Quarter whose four buildings illuminate as restored; the header shows timber, essence, and total ready output; the Quarter panel exposes live station rates/caps/affinities, collection, and resident slots; the Residents panel switches between all built workstations; Projects display mixed-resource costs and Keep rewards. Cache pins advanced to `keep.css?v=3` and `keep.js?v=4`; `render_game_to_text` now includes both currencies, all stations/affinities/residents, milestones, weekly tribute, and offline-overlay state.

- July 20, 2026 My Keep workshop crafting and interiors: replaced the shared Essence pool with four persistent crafted materials—Verdant Fiber, Ember Ingots, Frost Crystals, and Storm Cells—produced independently by the Garden, Forge, Fridge, and Generator. Once the Storehouse is restored, all four level-one blueprints are offered together so players choose their construction order; their output then cross-feeds level-two facilities, Storehouse expansion, resident-guided production tools, shared capacity/output bonuses, and one-time decorations. Added server-authoritative crafting and room-decoration placement endpoints with optimistic version/idempotency handling and Firestore persistence. Each elemental facility now opens into its own animated code-native interior, with a dedicated resident display and placeable Living Trellis, Emberglass Lantern, Frostglass Mobile, or Harmonic Orb; the Covenant Hall also gains storage/decoration recipes. The tutorial explains the choose-build-produce-craft loop. Original prompt: In the same way add a button that allows you to purchase instead of building projects for significant siegecoin it instantly buys the building or upgrade.

- July 28, 2026 Keep instant project purchases: every eligible build or upgrade in the Projects popup now offers a separate gold-styled Siegecoin action with an inline confirmation. Its server-owned price is deliberately substantial: 250 base coins, plus the project's timber cost, plus two coins per construction minute, plus eight coins per required workshop material. Purchasing skips timber/material costs, construction-team availability, and the timer, while preserving project prerequisites and Keeper-level requirements; the result immediately applies the building or upgrade, grants normal project XP and Enclave task credit, updates completed-project statistics, and leaves any already-running construction untouched. The authenticated `/api/keep/build/purchase` endpoint derives the price and validates and deducts the account's Siegecoins server-side, while build-option snapshots expose the quoted price and affordability. Test-mode simulation and `render_game_to_text` mirror the flow. The combined Keep purchase bundle lands on `keep.css?v=37` and `keep.js?v=38` after rebasing over the Keeper's Favor release.
- Verification: all 42 `KeepServiceTest` tests pass, including buying with a busy crew, exact account-coin deduction, zero timber/material spending, immediate building and upgrade effects, insufficient-funds rejection, and project statistics. The focused Keep static regression passes and the JavaScript parses successfully in the Node-backed runtime. Responsive browser testing at 390x844 and 1440x900 confirmed the ordinary build action remains disabled while the only crew is busy, the 710-Siegecoin Forge purchase remains enabled, the confirmation clearly explains all skipped costs, the Forge applies immediately, and the existing Fridge construction continues. Both viewports measured zero horizontal overflow and zero browser errors; the mobile and desktop states were visually inspected. TODO: none.
